  ## fidelity
  No comp, so this is judged against OWN-WORLD and DESIGN.md.
  - TYPE: match. Source Serif 4 is used for the verdict and headings, and Libre
  Franklin with tabular figures for everything else. Both are self-hosted via
  next/font.
  - MATERIAL: match. Hairline rules, no cards, no shadows, no faked physicality.
  The bordered segmented control, inputs and buttons are an acceptable
  adaptation: they are controls, not containers.
  - GROUND: match. The paper colour oklch(97.6% 0.009 84) is the warm off-white
  OWN-WORLD names. Ink and warm-grey secondary are as specified.
  - Accent: match. Ink-blue is used only for P1 squares, links, active
  nav/segment and the badge.
  - Signed numbers: match. Muted green/red appears on the number only (rows,
  tables, why-line).
  - Top bar, dateline, serif verdict, count line, 24h/7d/30d control, hairline
  rows, routine row at the end: match (mobile.png). Seven rows plus the routine
  row fit at 390.
  - Row line 2 "key fact · why ranked": acceptable adaptation, citing the 390px
  truncation evidence and the full reason kept in "Why it's here".
  - Signature rank-reason line: match on 30d. Boosted reasons are semibold, and
  the signed move is the row's only colour. On 24h every row reads "Reaction
  pending" in italic grey.
  - Line-1 category label: contradicted on the 30d view. "KPITTECH Mgmt chan…"
  (exp-a) truncates the "what" of the row. exp-a predates the 05:13 FeedRow
  change; verify on recapture.
  - Verdict line at 7d/30d: contradicted. "110 things matter this month." no
  longer reads as a verdict.
  - Expanded results card: match. Consolidated · ₹ crore, QoQ/YoY, one-off line,
  EBITDA definition, "Open filing at p. N".
  - Bank path (HDFCBANK): match. NII, Total income, PBT, PAT, plus the NII
  definition.
  - Mgmt-change card: match. Name, role, effective date, bio clamped with
  "Profile from the filing, p. 2 · Full profile".
  - Failed-card expansion (TRENT): match. The row still shows, with "Couldn't
  read this filing automatically" and the raw link.
  - Quiet, loading and failure states: match. The skeleton rows are a slight
  mismatch (see fix 7).
  - Alerts inbox: contradicted on row grammar. Line 1 reads "Filing" on every
  filing alert. The category moves into line 2 as a prefix, and the raw subject
  replaces the Brief's key fact.
  - Desktop: acceptable adaptation. A single centred reading column at 1440.

  ## ceiling
  - The 7d and 30d windows are one flat list of 110+ rows. A paper would section
  them by day with the heavy rule the Alerts inbox already uses. This is the
  world's most obvious unused tool.
  - The verdict is a bare count. The morning-note form allows a one-clause lede
  naming the top story (e.g. "Tata Power reported; Bajaj Finance closed its
  QIP"), built from data the feed already has.
  - The FINN nameplate is a plain 18px semibold serif. The masthead is the one
  place this world allows a stronger typographic commitment, and it is unused.

  ## material_fixes
  1. Verdict copy at 7d/30d (contract STORY/THESIS): "110 things matter this
  month." stops being a judgment. Make the windowed verdict count something that
  stays meaningful, such as boosted filings ("N moved their stock") with the
  material count in the sub-line, or phrase it as a count of material filings.
  2. Line-1 category must never be the element that truncates (DESIGN line 1;
  seen as "Mgmt chan…" on KPITTECH at 30d). Make the reason the element that
  yields or abbreviates, or shorten the label, so TICKER · category · marker
  always shows whole at 390.
  3. Results key fact mixes bases. "Rev +40.2% QoQ · PAT −48.7% YoY" (DIXON,
  SUNPHARMA) puts two different comparisons side by side and invites a misread
  (THESIS, read in one glance). Use one basis for both figures on the row; the
  table already carries the other.
  4. 24h first viewport: TATAPOWER "Results" appears three times, once with the
  P1 square and twice without, and every row's reason is "Reaction pending".
  Nothing on the row explains why rows 6–7 rank below row 2 (THESIS: "ordering
  explains itself on every row"). The line-1 label or the reason slot must say
  what the item is, e.g. "Results deck" or "Results · no figures".
  5. Alerts inbox: use the Brief's row grammar, with the category in the line-1
  slot in place of "Filing" and the same key fact. Today the TATAPOWER alert
  reads "Regulation 30 Change in Management" while the Brief row says "Sanjay
  Banga appointed" (STORY coherence).
  6. Settings spacing is inverted (craft floor, Spacing). In r3/settings.png,
  which postdates the fix, "Usually worth a look" sits about 10px under the
  Pledging rule and about 45px above "Earnings call", so it reads as a caption
  of Pledging. Put the space above the group label and keep it tight to its
  group.
  7. Loading skeleton (DESIGN key states: "skeleton rows that match the real
  layout"): it has a right block on line 1 only, but the shipped row now carries
  the filing time right-aligned on line 2. Add the line-2 right block.

  ## keep
  Keep the hairline 2-line row and the rank-reason slot where the signed move or
  volume multiple is the only colour. Keep the serif verdict and dateline over
  warm paper with a single ink-blue accent. Fixes must not add boxes, badges or
  colour to rows.

  Not checked: keyboard/focus behaviour and contrast measured from pixels; I
  judged contrast from the token values. Risk: four of the captures I scored
  predate the last two code edits, so fixes 2 and 7 need to be confirmed on
  fresh captures.

● Reviewer verdict: fix, with 7 material items. All are valid, and I'm applying
  them in one batch:

  1. The 7d/30d verdict counts companies with material news, not filings ("110
     things matter" isn't a judgment).
  2. The category label on line 1 never truncates; the market reason gives way
     instead.
  3. The results key fact uses one basis: YoY for both Rev and PAT.
  4. Secondary results filings (decks, re-filings without figures) are labelled
     "Results doc", so the ordering explains itself.
  5. The alerts inbox uses the Brief's row grammar: category on line 1, the same
     key fact on line 2.
  6. Settings group spacing: <legend> ignores fieldset padding, so the space
     landed on the wrong side.
  7. The skeleton gets the line-2 time block.

● The feed UI is built and running. Open this on your phone over the same Wi-Fi:

  http://10.87.143.228:3000

  If it doesn't load, Windows Firewall is probably blocking Node. Allow it on
  Private networks when Windows asks, or check that your phone isn't on a guest
  Wi-Fi network. I confirmed the laptop serves both the page and the data at
  that address; I haven't seen it load on a phone.

  What's built:
  - Brief (home): the date and "last checked" time, a serif verdict line ("3
    things matter today."), a counts line, the 24h/7d/30d picker, ranked 2-line
    rows, a collapsed routine row, and the alert badge on the nav.
  - Tap to expand:
    - Results: the table (this Q, QoQ, YoY), consolidated label, one-off line,
      EBITDA definition, and a link to the filing page.
    - Banks: NII and Total income instead of EBITDA.
    - Management change: name, role, start date, and a bio from the filing
      clamped to 3 lines with its page number.
    - Every item: a "Why it's here" line with the full rank reason.
  - Alerts inbox: grouped by day, with "New" markers. The badge works as you
    specified: on the first visit only the latest day counts as unseen, and
    opening the inbox clears it.
  - Settings: volume ×, move vs Nifty ±%, and category toggles, stored on the
    device. Changing a threshold re-ranks the feed and rebuilds the alerts
    straight away.
  - States: I forced each one in the browser and checked it at phone width.
    - Loading shows skeleton rows shaped like real rows.
    - A failed data load says what happened and offers "Try again".
    - The quiet day reads "Nothing material today.", shows the hidden routine
      count, and links to the last 7 days.
    - "Reaction pending" is quiet grey italic.
    - Filings whose numbers couldn't be read still show, with the original link.
  - TS port of rank(): passes the shared 9-case table, and so does Python. While
    porting I found that Python rounds 3.25 to "3.2" but JavaScript rounds it
    to "3.3", so the port copies Python and I added that case to the shared
    table so the two can't drift.

  Things to know:
  - 30-day ingest: I re-ran it for 30 days as you chose. It cost $0.049 across
    59 calls (total so far $0.131), and the new feed.json is committed.
  - Row layout: one change from DESIGN.md. Long rank reasons cut both halves of
    line 2, so the market reason now sits on line 1 at the right (bold when it
    lifted the rank) and line 2 holds the fact and the filing time. It's logged
    in DECISIONS.md.
  - Week and month headlines count companies ("Material news at 5 companies this
    week"), because "110 things matter" isn't a judgment.
  - Design review: a separate reviewer agent checked the screens and asked for 7
    fixes plus one regression; all are fixed and it signed off. That sign-off
    covers those fixes, not a fresh review of every screen.

  Commits: 9 in total: the brief first, then scaffold + rank port, data,
  screens, layout fix, docs, review fixes, and the regression fix. PLAN.md now
  has a short status section and DECISIONS.md a "Feed UI" section.

  Not done or not checked:
  - The motion pass, as you asked.
  - Filing alerts open the PDF rather than the in-app item.
  - No credibility chip yet (it waits for PvD), no PWA manifest, no dark mode.
  - I haven't tested on a real phone or checked keyboard focus.
  - The design skill also wants DESIGN.md rewritten from the finished build. I
    didn't touch your brief file; tell me whether to refresh, overwrite or merge
    it.
  - The rank reason is hidden entirely if its first part is wider than the space
    left on line 1; nothing in today's data does that.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Assessment A: FINN Brief, Alerts and Settings (Operate mode, 390px)

  **Is "notice board, not an app" right?** Yes. The data is not the problem.
  Four things cause it:
  1. **The feed shows filings, not companies or events.** Each row is one BSE
  filing. In the 24h view, 6 of 7 rows are TATAPOWER, and four of them (Results,
  2× "Results doc", "Press Release Titled "Financial Re…") describe one
  earnings event.
  2. **Line 2 is often the filing's raw title, cut off mid-word.** For example
  "Outcome of QIP 2026 - Special C…", or bare "Regulation 30 Capacity Addition".
  A notice board shows the paper as filed; FINN should say what it means.
  3. **Nothing looks tappable or app-like.** Rows have no chevron and no press
  state. Expanding a row snaps open with no transition. The nav is a
  website-style text header at the top. There is no web manifest (grep finds
  none), so the page always opens inside the browser's URL bar.
  4. **Alerts is literally a pinboard.** It is a date-grouped list of text with
  "Open filing" links stuck on the end of sentences, and the rows don't expand.

  **Design specificity verdict:** The voice and typography are specific to this
  product; the layout and interactions are generic. The serif headline, warm
  paper colour, Franklin with tabular figures, "vs Nifty", "PAT (to owners)",
  "Consolidated · ₹ crore" and "Why it's here" are clearly FINN's. But the page
  structure (header, h1, segmented control, flat list, fine-print footer) could
  belong to any feed app.

  **Forced dark (your phone screenshots):** The page never declares
  `color-scheme`, so Android Chrome's auto-dark repaints it. That loses the
  paper identity. The 7px P1 square (`bg-accent`, dark navy) becomes almost
  invisible on near-black (screenshots 1, 6). On the longest rows, the italic
  "Reaction pending" has its "R" clipped by `overflow-hidden` (screenshots 2, 5,
  6).

  ### Heuristic scores
  | # | Heuristic | Score | Key issue |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | System status | 3 | "Last checked 05:09 IST", skeleton and badge are
  good; nothing says whether the export is stale. |
  | 2 | Real-world match | 2 | Raw BSE titles ("Outcome of PI 2026 - Allotment
  Committee"), and "Results doc" is unexplained. |
  | 3 | User control | 2 | Window choice is in `useState`, so it resets to 24h
  after leaving the Brief. Opening Alerts marks everything seen, with no undo. |
  | 4 | Consistency | 2 | Brief rows expand; Alerts rows don't. Alerts labels
  volume-only crossings "Price". The P1 marker is missing on Alerts. The
  headline counts "things" at 24h but "companies" at 7d/30d. |
  | 5 | Error prevention | 3 | Number fields validate inline with a minimum. |
  | 6 | Recognition | 3 | Thresholds are repeated where needed; the P1 square is
  never explained on screen. |
  | 7 | Flexibility | 2 | No ticker filter, no mute, no grouping; the 30d view
  is about 160 flat rows. |
  | 8 | Minimalism | 2 | "Reaction pending" appears 7× in the strongest slot;
  the same ticker repeats 6×. |
  | 9 | Error recovery | 3 | Per-item fallback to the raw filing, plus retry.
  "Couldn't fetch filings for X" has no retry. |
  | 10 | Help | 2 | Only the footer explains ranking. "vs Nifty", the square and
  "Results doc" are undefined. |
  | **Total** | | **24/40** | **Acceptable** |

  ### Cognitive load: 4 failures (high)
  - **Visual hierarchy:** P1 and P2 rows have the same weight.
  - **Chunking:** the 30d list is flat.
  - **Grouping:** a company's related filings are scattered across the list.
  - **Minimal choices:** Settings shows 12 category checkboxes plus 2 inputs at
  once.

  ### Emotional journey
  - **Peak:** "3 things matter today." is confident.
  - **Valley:** the rows never show which 3. Matters and to-note are mixed, and
  every row is pending.
  - **End:** the scroll ends on footer fine print instead of a "you're caught
  up" close.
  - **30d deflates:** "Material news at 20 companies" covers all 20 holdings, so
  the filter admits it filtered nothing.

  ### What's working
  - **The results expansion.** It has basis and units, QoQ/YoY, the one-off
  gain, the EBITDA definition and "Open filing at p. 8". That is the
  traceability principle done properly.
  - **Rankings explain themselves on the row,** e.g. bold "+9.9% vs Nifty · 5.8×
  vol".
  - **Honest states.** "Couldn't read this filing automatically. The original is
  linked below.", the quiet state, and skeletons that match the real rows.

  ### Priority issues
  1. **[P1] The unit is the filing, not the company-event.**
     - *Why:* the PM has to piece one event together from 4 rows. The same
  pattern shows in BAJFINANCE fund raise ×3 and KPITTECH M&A ×2 with identical
  titles.
     - *Fix:* cluster by symbol + event family within 48h. Show one row, and
  list "+3 related filings" in the expanded view.
  2. **[P1] Line 2 shows the raw filing title.**
     - *Why:* the key fact gets truncated, and the row reads like a regulator's
  notice.
     - *Fix:* in `keyFact`, strip boilerplate prefixes with rules ("Outcome of",
  "Press Release Titled", "Regulation 30"). For P1/P2 rows without a card,
  require the cheap model's headline of 60 characters or less.
  3. **[P1] Forced dark destroys the identity and the P1 marker.**
     - *Fix:* add `color-scheme: only light` on `html` plus the matching meta
  tag. Or deliberately ship a "night paper" palette under
  `prefers-color-scheme`.
  4. **[P2] Hierarchy is flat.**
     - *Fix:* split the list into two labelled sections, "Matters (3)" and "To
  note (4)", to mirror the headline. When every row is pending, say "Market
  reaction after 16:30 IST" once in the header and drop it from the rows.
  5. **[P2] It doesn't feel like an app.**
     - *Fix:* add a manifest with `display: standalone`. Move the nav to a
  bottom tab bar under 640px. Give rows a press state (`bg-paper-2`), a small
  disclosure mark, and a 150ms grid-rows expand. Make Alerts reuse `FeedRow`.

  ### Persona red flags
  - **Casey (distracted, one-handed):** Brief/Alerts/Settings and the 24h/7d/30d
  chips sit in the top 50px, out of thumb reach. Window choice and the open row
  are lost on navigation. "Full profile" and "Open filing" are small inline
  text links. Thresholds need typing where a stepper would do.
  - **Alex (power user):** no ticker filter, no mute, no keyboard navigation
  (j/k), no expand-all, no deep link to an item. The 30d view is about 160
  ungrouped rows.
  - **Sam (accessibility):** line 1 is `whitespace-nowrap` with `shrink-0` on
  the ticker and label, so at 200% zoom "DEEPAKNTR Mgmt change" will overflow
  sideways. Results-table headers are 12px. Alerts marks items seen silently,
  with no live region. In forced dark, contrast is outside our control.
  - **The PM at 7am:** before the market opens, "Reaction pending" on every row
  is guaranteed and tells them nothing. They need "watch at 09:15". There is no
  "new since you last looked" marker on the Brief. The 3 material items aren't
  visually separated from the 4 to note.

  ### Minor observations
  - Use "Yesterday 18:54" instead of "11 Nov 18:54" in the 24h view.
  - "Bonus split" reads as one event.
  - The footer paragraph shows on every load; move it into a "How ranking works"
  disclosure.
  - Number-field saves on blur give no confirmation.
  - The quiet state leaves about 60% of the screen blank below it.
  - Alerts rows have no time of day.

  ### Questions to consider
  - What if the Brief were a list of companies with today's verdict, with the
  filings underneath?
  - At 7am, should the right-hand slot show the market reaction (always pending
  then) or "what to watch at open"?
  - Should 30d be a feed at all, or a per-company results scoreboard?

  I didn't inspect the live dev server or check forced-dark contrast
  numerically. The 200%-zoom overflow is read from the code, not observed.

  I did not measure the expanded-row controls ("Full profile", the source link);
  I didn't open any rows.

  **Color-scheme.** The page declares none. There is no `<meta
  name="color-scheme">`, the computed `color-scheme` on `:root` is `normal`, and
  the only colour meta is `theme-color #f8f5ef`. Headless Chrome reported
  `prefers-color-scheme: dark` as true, and the page has no dark styles. With
  nothing declared, Android Chrome's Auto Dark applies its force-dark algorithm,
  which matches what the user saw on their phone. `color-scheme: only light`
  (CSS) or `<meta name="color-scheme" content="only light">` would opt the page
  out. I also turned on Auto Dark emulation
  (`Emulation.setAutoDarkModeOverride`). The computed colours didn't change,
  because force-dark recolours at paint time and doesn't change the CSS values,
  so I couldn't measure the darkened ratios and took no screenshot.

  **False positives:**
  - **cream-palette** is a deliberate "Morning paper" choice, documented in a
  comment in `globals.css`. It's a taste flag, not a defect, and is reported
  once per route for the same `body` rule.
  - **straggler** depends on the data: the copy contains the live threshold
  values, so the line break moves with the numbers. Low severity.

  **Skipped or failed steps:**
  - I ran live-server from a scratch directory, not the repo root, so that
  starting it could not inject anything into project files. It ran on port 8400
  and I stopped it with `stop --keep-inject`.
  - The CDP script set the injected tag and title in the browser only; I made no
  project edits.
  - Chrome (debug port 9333 on 127.0.0.1) and its temp profile are gone,
  confirmed by a process and folder check.
  - `git status` shows changes to
  `backend/finn/{ingest,llm,sources/screener}.py` and new files
  `prompts/headline.md` and `push/headline.py`. Those were already there and are
  not mine.

        uarterly numbers for the quarters after that call, from the compa

› Message from general-purpose
  **Assessment A: design review of the FINN redesign (r6)**

  I worked from the source files and the r6 captures. I did not drive Chrome
  because the captures covered every state.

  **Design specificity verdict: authored, with some category-default parts.**
  The design belongs to this product: the warm paper palette, the serif verdict
  sentence that names companies ("Tata Power and Bajaj Finance need a look
  today."), ₹ crore figures, moves shown "vs Nifty", the "Results season: 18 of
  20 reported" line, page-cited filing links and the "That's everything for
  today." ending. The rich card's chrome is the generic fintech pattern: a
  rounded bordered card, then monogram, name and meta, then a sparkline. Groww
  or INDmoney could use it unchanged. The sparkline in the Today view is
  decoration, because the filing day falls after the last price bar, so no
  marker is drawn.

  **Prior P1s: all five resolved, one with leftovers.** The company is now the
  unit (`lib/group.ts`). The scheme is forced light (`color-scheme: only
  light`). The hierarchy has two tiers plus a verdict. It feels like an app: tab
  bar, PWA manifest, safe areas. Raw exchange titles are mostly fixed, but
  leftovers remain (Maruti, "Rs.", see Minor observations).

  **Heuristics: 28/40, Good (previously 24)**
  1. Visibility of system status, 3: "Last checked 06:05 IST" sits next to
  prices that are 3 days old. The banner is honest but undercuts the timestamp.
  2. Match with the real world, 3: the headlines are human now. The chip "3.0×
  vol" has no direction, and the Maruti alert is a raw exchange subject.
  3. User control and freedom, 3: the window choice persists and everything
  expands. Opening Alerts marks everything seen at once, and single alerts can't
  be dismissed.
  4. Consistency and standards, 2: Brief groups by company, but Alerts lists one
  row per filing (Tata Power appears twice on 11 Nov). Prices show as "Rs." in
  one place and "₹" in another. "Reaction pending" appears again in the expanded
  "Why it's here" line, against the "say it once" brief.
  5. Error prevention, 3: threshold inputs have minimums, steps and a Reset
  button.
  6. Recognition rather than recall, 3: "+2 more filings" is opaque; you have to
  open it to learn what's inside.
  7. Flexibility and efficiency, 2: an alert can't jump to its card, the 30-day
  view can't be sorted by mover, and Research is a placeholder.
  8. Aesthetic and minimalist design, 3: calm and dense. The routine count
  appears twice per screen.
  9. Error recognition and recovery, 3: the failure state names the error,
  offers a retry and points to BSE/NSE. But it says "check your connection" for
  an HTTP 503, which is a server fault.
  10. Help and documentation, 3: "How ranking works", "Why it's here" and
  Settings copy all explain themselves.

  **Cognitive load: 2 checklist failures (moderate)**
  - **Working memory:** "6 moved ±3% vs Nifty" makes you scan 20 cards to find
  which six.
  - **Progressive disclosure:** it goes too far. A P1 item can be folded behind
  "+N more" with no label.
  - No decision point shows more than 4 options.

  **Emotional journey**
  - **Strong start:** the verdict sentence and the date-plus-time line feel calm
  and confident.
  - **Valley right after:** the grey "Prices are in up to 9 Nov" box is the
  second thing you read on 12 Nov.
  - **Peak:** the results card, with ₹ figures and green YoY numbers, then the
  expanded table with a citation.
  - **Second valley:** tapping an alert drops you out of the app into a BSE PDF.
  - **Good ending:** "That's everything for today." is a satisfying close.

  **Strengths**
  1. **The verdict headline is the product's voice.** It changes with context:
  named companies, a count, or a results-season tally.
  2. **Honest states:** the skeleton is shaped like the real card, the failure
  state is specific, the quiet state offers a wider window, and failed tickers
  are listed by name.
  3. **Disciplined colour:** colour appears only on signed numbers, and a single
  ink-blue accent avoids clashing with the loss red. Contrast for secondary
  text (ink-2 on paper) is about 5.5:1.

  **Priority issues**

  1. **[P1] A material management change is buried inside the results card.**
     - **Why:** Tata Power appointed Sanjay Banga as CEO, and Alerts flags it as
  New. In Brief it sits unlabelled behind "+2 more filings", because `ORDER` in
  `lib/group.ts` puts results first and the card shows only the lead. A CEO
  change is exactly what a 7am reader must not miss.
     - **Fix:** in `CompanyCard` (`components/Company.tsx`), when `g.more` holds
  a P1, add one line under the headline: "Also: Mgmt change · Appointed Sanjay
  Banga as CEO…" using `keyFact()`. The More button should then read "+2 more
  filings · incl. Mgmt change". Add a case to `group.test.ts`.
  2. **[P1] The card toggle is one button holding the whole card (Sam).**
     - **Why:** the `<button>` in `CompanyCard` wraps the `<dl>` from `Numbers`
  and the `<div>`s from `Spark`. That is invalid nesting, and the button's
  accessible name becomes the entire card. The Alerts `<a aria-label="TATAPOWER
  Results: open the filing">` overrides the visible headline, so screen readers
  never hear the actual fact.
     - **Fix:** make a smaller element the toggle: the headline or the "Details"
  row, with `aria-labelledby` pointing at the headline. Keep the visual tap
  area with an `::after` overlay. On the Alerts link, drop `aria-label` or
  append the fact text to it.
  3. **[P2] Alerts lead out of the app.**
     - **Why:** every alert row links straight to the BSE PDF
  (`app/alerts/page.tsx`). The rich card, filing-only bio, results table and
  "Why it's here" are all skipped, which hurts on 3G.
     - **Fix:** link to `/?w=…#c-SYMBOL` and open that card there. Keep the PDF
  link inside the card's Detail.
  4. **[P2] The 30-day ranking doesn't lead with the movers.**
     - **Why:** scores in `rank.ts` are coarse (300 or 450), so ties fall back
  to date. CDSL at "3.0× vol" ranks above Reliance at "+9.9% vs Nifty · 5.8×
  vol", and the "6 moved" claim can't be acted on.
     - **Fix:** in the `group()` sort, break ties by `|excess_pct|` and then
  `vol_ratio`. Make "6 moved…" in `verdict()` a button that filters to those
  companies.
  5. **[P3] Stale prices are framed as a caveat.**
     - **Fix:** fold the price date into the meta line ("Prices to 9 Nov") and
  drop the boxed banner. On Today cards, draw a dashed "filed" tick past the
  line's right end so the sparkline still tells you something.

  **Persona red flags**
  - **Casey (one-handed, on the move):**
    - The window toggle is at the top of the screen, out of thumb reach
  (persistence softens this).
    - The "Details" cue is 12px grey text.
    - Tab labels are 11px.
    - An alert tap means a PDF download on mobile data.
  - **Sam (screen reader):**
    - Each card announces as one huge button.
    - The alert link labels hide the headline.
    - "Material" is only a 7px square on routine rows (it does have
  `aria-label`).
  - **The 7am portfolio manager:**
    - Misses the Tata Power CEO appointment.
    - Has to hunt for the six movers.
    - Trusts "Last checked 06:05" until the banner says prices stop at 9 Nov.
    - Sees "Maruti · Results: Reference to the Financial Results issued by
  Suzuki Motor Corporation" as a material alert. That is the parent company's
  results, misclassified, and it costs credibility.

  **Minor observations**
  - "5 routine filings hidden" appears in both the subline and the bottom row.
  In the quiet state, "137" appears twice.
  - "Reaction pending" shows in the expanded "Why it's here: results · Reaction
  pending" line.
  - Model-written titles mix "Rs. 1,010" with "₹992.25". Normalise to ₹ in
  ingest.
  - Read the "Rs." vs "₹" mix as one sign of uneven title quality across the
  ingest output.
  - Chips like "3.0× vol" carry no price direction; "Flat · 3.0× vol" would read
  better.
  - The 503 message should say "FINN's data server didn't respond", not "check
  your connection".
  - The FINN wordmark and the tab icons are consistent and drawn for the app
  (one 1.75 stroke). Good.

  **Questions to consider**
  - Should a company with two P1 filings in one window get two headlines rather
  than one?
  - Is "Today" the right default when prices always lag by a day?

  I didn't check keyboard focus order live, didn't measure contrast in the
  browser, and didn't test the PWA install flow. The Maruti misclassification is
  a backend/classifier issue; the UI only exposes it.

› Message from general-purpose
  **Assessment B (detector and browser evidence) for FINN web**

  **1. CLI scan**
  - I ran `impeccable detect --json web/app web/components`. It scanned 11 tsx
  files, 1 ts, 1 svg and 1 css.
  - Result: exit 0, output `[]`. That is **0 findings** and no rules triggered.

  **2. Browser overlay**
  - Setup: headless Chrome 157 with CDP on 127.0.0.1:9351, a fresh profile, a
  390x844 mobile viewport at DPR 3, and touch emulation.
  - On every route, the page title and script-append test passed, so the page
  could be modified. `detect.js` from the live server on port 8400 loaded.
  - Each of the four routes (`/`, `/alerts`, `/settings`, `/research`) reported
  the same thing: `[impeccable] 1 anti-pattern found`. The rule was
  **cream-palette** ("cream/beige page background rgb(250, 247, 240)") on
  `body.min-h-dvh`.
  - On `/` the first pass logged one "Failed to load resource: 404". A second
  pass with network logging saw no 4xx responses, so it looks transient
  (probably HMR or a favicon). I did not find out which URL it was.

  **3. Stale CSS on the dev server (most important)**
  The CSS chunk the dev server is serving is older than the current
  `web/app/globals.css`:
  - It has **no `--color-card` token and no `.bg-card` class**.
  - It has **no `color-scheme` rule**.

  What this does in the page:
  - The computed background of the card `li` and of the active window button is
  `rgba(0,0,0,0)`. Cards are rendering transparent on paper, not on the card
  colour.
  - `:root` computed `color-scheme` is **"normal"**, not "only light".
  - The `<meta name="color-scheme" content="only light">` tag is present, and
  `theme-color` is `#f8f5ef`.

  The dev server probably needs a restart or cache clear before the
  colour-scheme and card-background checks mean anything. I did not touch it.
  The colours that are served still match the source oklch tokens, which I
  checked by rendering them through a canvas.

  **4. Contrast (WCAG, measured on `/`, Today and 30-day windows)**

  | Element | fg / bg | Ratio |
  |---|---|---|
  | Body ink on paper | #1f1915 / #faf7f0 | 16.25 |
  | ink-2 on paper | | 6.72 (13px, 12px, 11px) |
  | ink-2 on paper-2/60 (window buttons) | | 6.38 |
  | ink-2 on paper-2 ("Prices are in up to…" banner, 13px) | | **6.15** (lowest
  measured) |

  | Tab labels, inactive, 11px | | 6.72 |
  | Ink on card (from source tokens) | | 17.09 |
  | ink-2 on card (from source tokens) | | 7.06 |
  | Gain on card (from source tokens) | | 6.94 |
  | Loss on card (from source tokens) | | 7.20 |

  Every pair passes AA (4.5:1). Every pair except the gain and loss figures also
  reaches AAA (7:1).

  **5. Touch targets (width x height in px)**

  | Target | Size | Result |
  |---|---|---|
  | Window buttons (Today / 7 days / 30 days) | 115x**40** | **Below 44** |
  | Tab-bar links | 98x56 | OK |
  | Header wordmark | 96x44 | OK |
  | Card buttons | 348x243–299 | OK |
  | "+N more filings" | 316x44 | OK |
  | "Show 14 more companies" | 350x44 | OK |
  | Routine "Show" row | 350x44 | OK |
  | Inline "Change thresholds" link | 114x16 | Inline text link, so normally
  exempt |

  **6. Manifest and icons**
  - `/manifest.webmanifest`: 200, `application/manifest+json`, display
  **standalone**, start_url `/`, theme and background colour `#f8f5ef`.
  - `icon-192.png`, `icon-512.png` and `maskable-512.png` (purpose maskable)
  each return 200 `image/png`.
  - `/icon.svg` returns 200 `image/svg+xml`.
  - The apple-touch-icon link is present.

  **7. Horizontal overflow**
  - At 390px there is no overflow, in either window.
  - At 195px on the Today window there is no overflow (scrollWidth 195 =
  clientWidth 195).
  - **At 195px on the 30-day window it overflows**: scrollWidth 292 against
  clientWidth 195.
  - With the root font set to 200% at 390px it also overflows: scrollWidth 580
  against 390.
  - The overflowing elements are in the "Also worth a look" rows: the `shrink-0
  text-[0.75rem] text-ink-2` date and chip spans, plus `text-gain` / `text-loss`
  in `Signed`. These sit in a `flex items-baseline` line with `ml-auto
  shrink-0` items that cannot wrap.

  **8. False positives**
  - **cream-palette** is deliberate. The `globals.css` comment says the "Morning
  paper" warm-paper palette is intended. Treat it as a style call, not a
  defect.
  - The 114x16 "Change thresholds" link is inline in a sentence, so the
  target-size rule does not apply.

  **9. Skipped or limited**
  - No real text-zoom emulation was available. I used a 195px viewport and a
  200% root font size instead.
  - I did not exercise the Alerts and Settings controls beyond injecting the
  overlay.
  - No loss numbers appeared in the 24h window, so the loss contrast comes from
  the 30-day window.
  - "Also worth a look" row buttons did not match my selector, so I have no
  measurement of their height.

  through CDP, and the temp profile was deleted. No project files changed; `git
  status` is the same as at the start. The scripts and raw results are in

  for grounds; ink and ink-2 for text; rule for hairlines; a single ink-blue

  tints are derived from the sector name (8 hues), never assigned per ticker.
  2. **Type:** Source Serif 4 is used only for judgement sentences: the verdict
  and page titles (1.75rem/600/-0.015em), section heads (1.1875rem) and the
  wordmark. Everything else is Libre Franklin with tabular figures: card
  headline 1.0625rem/600, body 0.875rem, meta 0.8125rem in ink-2, label 0.75rem,
  tab labels 0.6875rem.

  rhythm, 44px minimum targets). The company is the unit. "What matters" cards
  are capped at 6 (monogram, headline, "Also:" line, two figures, reaction chip,
  30-day sparkline with the filing day marked); "Also worth a look" uses
  compact rows; the old 2-line filing row lives on inside folds and the routine
  list. Also recorded: window control, secondary button, number field, bottom
  tab bar with badge, PWA, and the FINN mark.
  4. **Named rules:** Number-Only, One Ink, Paper Stays Paper (forced light),
  Serif Judges, Weight Not Colour, Paper Has No Shadows. The one recorded shadow
  exception is the 1px lift on the selected window segment.
  5. **States and motion:** card- and row-shaped skeletons, a single "Reaction
  pending" banner, a quiet-day verdict with a "widen window" link, and a serif
  failure title with "Try again". Motion is stated as not done yet; the planned
  pass is in DESIGN.md and as a "none-yet" token in the sidecar.

  (.impeccable/surfaces/web-app-page-tsx.md), stated in the DESIGN.md Overview
  and Components:

  boxed cards".

  - Alerts moved from the top bar to the bottom tab bar; the top bar now holds
  only the wordmark.

● The redesign and the Promised vs Delivered (PvD) backend are both built,
  committed and pushed to GitHub. The dev server is still running at
  http://10.87.143.228:3000. The Brief now loads with one card per company, so
  pull to refresh on your phone.

  The redesign

  Critique of the old build: 24/40. The reviewer agreed with your "notice board"
  read:
  - It showed one row per filing, so Tata Power appeared 6 times for one results
    event.
  - Line 2 was the raw exchange subject, cut off mid-word.
  - Nothing declared a colour scheme, which is why Chrome force-darkened it.
  - Every row carried the same weight, and it had no app shell.

  What I built, by your priority list:
  - Forced light (0): color-scheme: only light in both CSS and the meta tag. I
    checked it's what the page actually computes.
  - One entry per company (1): the lead item follows your order (results, then
    management change, then fund raise…). Results decks and outcome letters fold
    into the results item, and other filings fold behind "+N more filings".
    - A fix from the second critique: a material filing that isn't the lead now
      shows as an "Also:" line. Otherwise Tata Power's new CEO was hidden under
      its results.
  - Two tiers (2): "What matters" cards have:
    - a monogram avatar tinted by the company's sector (taken from screener's
      sector label, so nothing is hard-coded per ticker);
    - a human headline;
    - Revenue and PAT with YoY;
    - a reaction chip next to a 30-day sparkline, with the filing day marked.

    Cards cap at 6, with "Show N more companies" after that. "Also worth a look"
    uses compact rows, and routine filings stay collapsed.
  - Human headlines (3): the cheap model writes ≤12 words per filing, numbers
    copied as printed. 107 of 109 filings got one. It cost $0.044 for 98 calls.
    Results headlines come from the card's numbers (no LLM), and anything that
    fails falls back to the subject with its boilerplate stripped.
  - "Reaction pending" said once (4): a single banner, "Prices are in up to 9
    Nov…", instead of on every row.
  - App feel (5): bottom tab bar (Brief, Alerts, Research, Settings) with icons
    I drew, safe-area padding, and a PWA manifest set to open standalone.
    Research is an honest placeholder for now.
  - Logo (6): an "F" made of ranked bars, with the ink-blue square as the third
    row; it doubles as the marker for important filings. It's used for the
    header, app icon and favicon.
  - Verdict headline (7): "Results season: 18 of 20 reported." with "6 moved ±3%
    vs Nifty on results" underneath. The Today view names companies: "Tata
    Power and Bajaj Finance need a look today."

  Critique at 390px after the redesign: 28/40 (Good). Fixed from it:
  - Only the headline is now the tap target (the whole card is still tappable),
    so screen readers get a sensible name and the HTML is valid.
  - Tapping an alert opens that company's card in the app instead of a PDF.
  - At 30 days, bigger movers rank first instead of falling back to date order.
  - Window buttons are now 44px tall.
  - Rows wrap at 200% text zoom instead of spilling sideways.
  - "Rs." and "INR" display as ₹.
  - The server-error message no longer says "check your connection".

  The reviewer's other ideas I didn't take: folding the price-date banner into
  the date line (you asked for a banner), and making "6 moved" a filter.

  DESIGN.md has been rewritten to match the build. It states where the build
  departs from your original brief (boxed cards, the sparkline, one small shadow
  on the selected window button). PLAN.md has status sections 3e and 3f, and
  DECISIONS.md has the trade-offs.

  Promised vs Delivered backend

  - How it works: it picks the 4 latest results calls. A call 1–2 months after a
    quarter end counts; that drops TCS's December analyst day and HDFCBANK's
    "Mar 2026" call. When screener lists two transcripts for one quarter, the
    BSE copy wins, because its TCS "Jul 2026" company-site link is actually the
    2025 file. The cheap model pulls promises with a verbatim quote and page
    number. Python attaches actuals from screener's 13-quarter table and
    computes all growth figures. The strong model only gives the verdict.
  - Results:
    - TCS: 5 promises, 1 hit ("25%+ exit margin" → 26% in Sep 2026), and 4 not
      measurable yet.
    - HDFCBANK: 3 promises, all not measurable yet. They're FY27 targets for
      CASA and loan-to-deposit ratios, and screener has no rows for those.
  - How to judge these results: the answers are honest but thin. TCS gives no
    numeric revenue guidance, and the bank's promises can't be checked against
    screener's table. This is the "is PvD quality good enough?" question already
    planned for Friday. My suggestion: use our own extracted results cards as
    the actuals.
  - Strong model availability: it was often overloaded, so the fallback model
    gave some verdicts, and the JSON says so.
  - Tests: 64 backend tests and 11 web tests pass.
  - Cost: PvD was $0.107 (≈ $0.05 per company). Total LLM spend so far: $0.29
    across about 222 calls.

  Questions skipped: you already set the priority order and scope (items 0–7),
  so I didn't ask the critique's usual follow-up questions.

  Not done or not checked:
  - The motion pass, as you asked.
  - Installing it as an app on a real phone (the manifest and icons are served
    correctly).
  - Maruti's note on its parent company's results is still wrongly marked as an
    important results filing.

       95  def run(task: str, prompt_name: str, inputs: list, schema: dic
           t, key: str | None = None, patient: bool = False, **log) -> di
           ct:
       96      """inputs: strings and/or PDF bytes. Returns {"data", "mod
           el", "model_note", "cached"}.
       97 -    patient: for precomputed outputs (batch PvD, bull/bear). K
          -eeps retrying the routed model (~10 min worst case)           
       98 -    and raises instead of falling back, so stored verdicts are
          - never the fallback model's (user's rule, 12 Nov).            
       97 +    Order: the task's model, then (strong tasks) STRONG_FALLBA
          +CK, then (live path only) the fast FALLBACK.                  
       98 +    Every answer records the model that produced it; anything 
          +but the primary also gets a model_note.                       
       99 +    patient: for precomputed outputs (batch PvD, bull/bear). R
          +etries each strong model longer and never drops to            
      100 +    the fast model; raises if both strong models stay down.

           s) when the input bytes themselves aren't
      102      stable; PyMuPDF page subsets differ byte-wise between runs
           . Extra keyword args go into the cost log."""
      103      text = prompt(prompt_name)

● Writing the new pvd.py. Python decides every verdict it can compute; the

      154 +def span(p: dict, call_q: str, periods: list[str]) -> tuple[li
          +st[str], list[str], str] | None:                              
      155 +    """(quarters the target covers, of those the ones reported
          +, label). None if the period can't be placed.                 
      156 +    'Rolling' targets (next few quarters, unspecified) cover t
          +he quarters since the call; long-term ones use the latest.""" 
      157 +    h, fy = p["horizon"], p.get("fy")                         
      158 +    if h == "next_quarter":                                   
      159 +        want = [shift(call_q, 1)]                             
      160 +    elif h == "quarter" and fy and p.get("quarter_no") in (1, 
          +2, 3, 4):                                                     
      161 +        want = [qlabel(fy, p["quarter_no"])]                  
      162 +    elif h == "half_year" and fy and p.get("half_no") in (1, 2
          +):                                                            
      163 +        want = [qlabel(fy, k) for k in ((1, 2) if p["half_no"]
          + == 1 else (3, 4))]                                           
      164 +    elif h == "fiscal_year" and fy:                           
      165 +        want = [qlabel(fy, k) for k in (1, 2, 3, 4)]          
      166 +    elif h in ("next_few_quarters", "unspecified"):           
      167 +        have = [q for q in periods if _q(q) > _q(call_q)]     
      168 +        return (have, have, f"Since the {fy_name(call_q)} call
          + ({', '.join(have)})") if have else None                      
      169 +    elif h == "long_term" and periods:                        
      170 +        return [periods[-1]], [periods[-1]], f"Latest quarter 
          +({periods[-1]}), against a long-term target"                  
      171 +    else:                                                     
      172 +        return None                                           
      173 +    have = [q for q in want if q in periods]                  
      174 +    if not have:                                              
      175 +        return None                                           
      176 +    name = f"FY{fy}" if h == "fiscal_year" else f"H{p['half_no
          +']} FY{fy}" if h == "half_year" else fy_name(want[0])         
      177 +    if len(have) == len(want):                                
      178 +        return want, have, f"{name} ({have[0]}{' – ' + have[-1
          +] if len(have) > 1 else ''})"                                 
      179 +    return want, have, f"{name} so far ({len(have)} of {len(wa
          +nt)} quarters: {', '.join(have)})"                            
      180 +                                                              
      181 +                                                              
      182 +def progress(p: dict, call_q: str, quarters: dict) -> dict | N
          +one:                                                          
      183 +    """Python verdict for a growth or margin target the quarte
          +rly table can measure; None otherwise."""                     
      184 +    lo, hi = p.get("target_low"), p.get("target_high")        
      185 +    if lo is None or p.get("basis") == "constant_currency_or_u
          +sd" or re.search(r"qoq|sequential|quarter-on-quarter", p["targ
          +et"], re.I):                                                  
      186 +        return None  # ₹ figures can't check a constant-curren
          +cy or sequential target                                       
      187 +    s = span(p, call_q, quarters.get("periods", []))          
      188 +    if not s:                                                 
      189 +        return None                                           
      190 +    want, have, label = s                                     
      191 +    if p["target_type"] == "growth_pct" and p["metric_key"] in
          + ROWS:                                                        
      192 +        row = _row(quarters, ROWS[p["metric_key"]])           
      193 +        prev = [row.get(shift(q, -4)) for q in have] if row el
          +se []                                                         
      194 +        if not row or None in prev or not sum(prev):          
      195 +            return None                                       
      196 +        v = (sum(row[q] for q in have) / sum(prev) - 1) * 100 
      197 +        what = f"{'Revenue' if p['metric_key'] == 'revenue' el
          +se 'Net profit'} {v:+.1f}% YoY"                               
      198 +    elif p["target_type"] == "margin_pct" and p["metric_key"] 
          +in MARGIN:                                                    
      199 +        pair = next(((n, d) for n, d in MARGIN[p["metric_key"]
          +] if _row(quarters, [n]) and _row(quarters, [d])), None)      
      200 +        if not pair:                                          
      201 +            return None                                       
      202 +        num, den = _row(quarters, [pair[0]]), _row(quarters, [
          +pair[1]])                                                     
      203 +        if any(q not in num or q not in den for q in have):   
      204 +            return None                                       
      205 +        v = sum(num[q] for q in have) / sum(den[q] for q in ha
          +ve) * 100  # revenue-weighted, not an average of %s           
      206 +        what = f"{'Operating' if p['metric_key'] == 'operating
          +_margin' else 'Net'} margin {v:.1f}%"                         
      207 +    else:                                                     
      208 +        return None                                           
      209 +    goal = f"{lo:g}–{hi:g}%" if hi is not None else f"≥{lo:g}%
          +"                                                             
      210 +    if len(have) == len(want) and p["horizon"] not in ("next_f
          +ew_quarters", "unspecified", "long_term"):                    
      211 +        verdict = "hit" if v >= lo else "miss"                
      212 +    else:                                                     
      213 +        verdict = "ahead" if hi is not None and v > hi else "o
          +n track" if v >= lo else "behind pace"                        
      214 +    return {"verdict": verdict, "actual": f"{label}: {what} vs
          + target {goal}", "actual_source": SOURCE, "model": RULE,      
      215 +            "why": f"{what} against {goal}{'' if len(have) == 
          +len(want) else ' with the period still running'}; computed in 
          +Python."}                                                     
      216 +                                                              
      217 +                                                              
      218 +# ---- later statements as evidence for what the table can't m
          +easure ----                                                   
      219 +_words = lambda s: {w for w in re.findall(r"[a-z]{3,}", s.lowe
          +r())} - {"the", "and", "for", "with", "our", "ratio", "growth"
          +}

      222 +def relevant(p: dict, reported: list[dict], k: int = 8) -> lis
          +t[dict]:                                                      
      223 +    """Later-call statements most likely about this promise (w
          +ord overlap), so each verdict call stays small:               
      224 +    Groq's free tier allows 8k tokens a minute."""            
      225 +    later = [x for x in reported if _q(x["said_in"]) > _q(p["s
          +aid_in"])]                                                    
      226 +    want = _words(p["metric"] + " " + p["target"])            
      227 +    scored = [(len(want & _words(x["metric"] + " " + x["value"
          +])) + (x["metric_key"] == p["metric_key"] != "other"), x) for 
          +x in later]                                                   
      228 +    return [x for s, x in sorted(scored, key=lambda t: -t[0]) 
          +if s > 0][:k]

         -arget`, `period`, `quote`), the quarter of the call it was said
         - on (`said_in`), `actuals` (reported quarterly figures for the 
         -quarters after that call, from the exchange results filings: va
         -lues in ₹ crore or % for margins; `yoy_pct`, `qoq_pct` and `*_p
         -p` are already computed), and `eligible_reported`: the ids of `
         -reported` entries said on calls after this promise.            
       3 +You get JSON: `latest_quarter` (the latest quarter with reporte
         +d results) and `promises`. Each promise has an `id`, what was p
         +romised (`metric`, `target`, `period`, `quote`), the call it wa
         +s made on (`said_in`, e.g. "Q4 FY26"), `actuals` (reported quar
         +terly figures after that call, values in ₹ crore or % for margi
         +ns, with `yoy_pct` / `qoq_pct` / `*_pp` already computed), and 
         +`reported`: numbers management stated as achieved on later call
         +s, each with an `id` like "R4", the call it was said on, the `v
         +alue` exactly as stated and a verbatim `quote`.                
       4  
       5  For each promise return:
       6  - `verdict`: one of
      10 -  - `hit`: the evidence meets or beats the target for the state
         -d period.                                                      
       7 +  - `hit`: the evidence meets or beats the target, and the targ
         +et's period has ended or the target is already met.            
       8    - `miss`: the period has ended and the evidence clearly falls
           short.
      12 -  - `partial`: some of it was delivered (part of the period, pa
         -rt of the amount, or a range only partly reached).             
      13 -  - `not-yet-measurable`: the period has not ended by `latest_q
         -uarter`, or no evidence given measures this promise.           
      14 -- `used_quarters`: the quarter labels from `actuals` you relied
         - on.                                                           
      15 -- `used_reported`: the ids from `eligible_reported` you relied 
         -on. Never an id that is not in that promise's `eligible_reporte
         -d`.                                                            
       9 +  - `partial`: part of it was delivered (part of the amount, so
         +me of the items, a range only partly reached).                 
      10 +  - `not-yet-measurable`: no evidence given speaks to this prom
         +ise, or the period has not ended and nothing settles it yet.   
      11 +- `used_reported`: the ids from that promise's own `reported` l
         +ist you relied on (empty if none).                             
      12  - `why`: at most 25 words, citing the evidence numbers exactly 
          as given.
      13  
      14  Rules:
      15  - Use only the JSON. No outside knowledge about the company, it
          s sector or later events.
      20 -- Do not compute anything. Compare the given numbers with the t
         -arget; if a judgment needs a number that is not given, the verd
         -ict is not-yet-measurable.                                     
      21 -- An annual target (e.g. "FY27") cannot be judged on part of th
         -e year unless the evidence already settles it (e.g. a full-year
         - capacity already commissioned).                               
      22 -- Prefer `actuals` for revenue, margin and profit; use `reporte
         -d` for everything else.

         +reported` constant-currency or USD number, never from the ₹ `ac

          +]:

          +arget's bounds and direction. Finished period -> hit/miss;

      251      return [x for s, x in sorted(scored, key=lambda t: -t[0])

      254 +def pace_reported(p: dict, used: list[dict], latest: str | Non
          +e) -> dict | None:                                            
      255 +    """A % level target (LDR 85-90% by FY27, CASA ~38%) checke
          +d against the newest % management reported for it:            
      256 +    Python parses that number and decides; the model only chos
          +e which statement is the evidence."""                         
      257 +    if p["target_type"] not in ("margin_pct", "other") or (p.g
          +et("target_low") is None and p.get("target_high") is None) or 
          +"%" not in p["target"]:                                       
      258 +        return None                                           
      259 +    newest = max(used, key=lambda x: _q(x["said_in"]))        
      260 +    nums = re.findall(r"(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)\s*%", newest["value"])
      261 +    if len(nums) != 1:                                        
      262 +        return None  # zero or several numbers: can't tell whi
          +ch one is the level                                           
      263 +    v = float(nums[0])                                        
      264 +    ends = {"fiscal_year": p.get("fy") and qlabel(p["fy"], 4),
          + "half_year": p.get("fy") and p.get("half_no") and qlabel(p["f
          +y"], 2 * p["half_no"]),                                       
      265 +            "quarter": p.get("fy") and p.get("quarter_no") and
          + qlabel(p["fy"], p["quarter_no"]), "next_quarter": shift(p["sa
          +id_in"], 1)}.get(p["horizon"])                                
      266 +    complete = bool(ends and latest and _q(ends) <= _q(latest)
          + and _q(newest["said_in"]) >= _q(ends))                       
      267 +    verdict, goal = judge(v, p, complete)                     
      268 +    return {"verdict": verdict, "why": f"Reported {newest['val
          +ue']} ({fy_name(newest['said_in'])} call) against {goal}; comp
          +ared in Python."}                                             
      269 +                                                              
      270 +                                                              
      271  def evidence(p: dict, reported: list[dict], used: list[str]) -
           > list[str]:
      272      """Management's own later statements the verdict relied on
           , as "Q2 FY27 call, p.6: “…”". Ids are checked against
      273      what this promise was allowed to use, so the model can't c
           ite a statement from before the promise."""

● Update(backend\finn\prompts\extract_guidance.md)

      15  - `fy`: the fiscal year as 2 digits (FY27 -> 27). "This year" m
          eans the fiscal year the call's quarter belongs to; "next year"
           the one after. Null if not a fiscal-year-bound target.
      16  - `quarter_no` (1-4) for a named quarter, `half_no` (1-2) for a
           half; null otherwise.
      17  - `basis`: constant_currency_or_usd if the target is stated in 
          constant currency, CC, USD or dollar terms; reported_inr for ru
          pee or reported figures; not_applicable otherwise.
      18 +- `direction`: at_least (the number is a floor: "25% plus", "do
         +uble-digit", "inch closer to 26-28%" from below), at_most (a ce
         +iling: "credit cost below 1%", "bring LDR down to 85-90%" from 
         +above), within (stay inside a band).                           
      19 +- `margin_basis` for margin targets: ebit (EBIT margin; IT-serv
         +ices companies' "operating margin" is EBIT, after depreciation)
         +, ebitda (EBITDA or "operating margin" before depreciation), ne
         +t (net profit margin), unclear; not_applicable for non-margin t
         +argets.                                                        
      20 +- Never take a promise from a question. If the quote is someone
         + asking about a target, find management's own words in the answ
         +er or skip it.                                                 
      21  
      22  **reported**: numbers management states as achieved, for the qu
          arter just ended or as of a date: growth rates (including const
          ant-currency growth), margins, ratios (CASA, loan-to-deposit, N
          IM, credit cost, GNPA), capacity or store counts, capex spent, 
          deal wins, segment revenue.
      23  - Only management's own statements of achieved results, never t
          argets, never analysts' numbers.

          n the prompt** (found by audit, 12 Nov: 24 of 107 titles ran ov

          ad). A title must be ≤12 words and every number in it must appe

          problem; otherwise the app shows the cleaned subject. Applies t

      86 +- **PvD verdicts are computed in Python wherever a number exist
         +s (12 Nov, user's rule: the hero feature must not be mostly "no
         +t yet measurable").** The extractor turns each target into numb
         +ers (low/high bound, direction, fiscal period, margin basis) an
         +d copies them; it does no maths. Python then:                  
      87 +  - maps the period to Indian fiscal quarters (FY27 = Jun 2026 
         +... Mar 2027) and measures against screener's quarterly table: 
         +growth = summed quarters vs the same quarters a year earlier; m
         +argins revenue-weighted, on the basis management used (IT servi
         +ces guide on **EBIT** margin = operating profit - depreciation;
         + others EBITDA);                                               
      88 +  - finished period -> **hit / miss**; running period -> **on t
         +rack / ahead / behind pace**, always with the numbers ("FY27 so
         + far (2 of 4 quarters): ...");                                 
      89 +  - "next few quarters" guidance from an older call is checked 
         +against the quarters since that call;                          
      90 +  - percentage-level targets the table can't hold (CD ratio, CA
         +SA) are checked against the newest % management reported on a l
         +ater call: the model only points at the statement, Python parse
         +s the number and decides.

  ⎿  (timeout 2m)

● All four decisions are done and pushed. TCS now has real verdicts (2 of 3
  measured, both "behind pace"). HDFCBANK is still all "not yet measurable": its
  later calls never state the numbers needed. All 82 tests pass, and the run
  finished without errors.

  1. Model: Groq openai/gpt-oss-120b is now the primary for PvD verdicts and
     bull/bear, with gemini-3.8-flash as fallback. Every verdict records the
     model that made it. Groq's free tier allows 1,000 requests a day and 8,000
     tokens a minute (read from its response headers). I keep each call under 5k
     tokens and wait when Groq asks. This is logged in DECISIONS.md.
  2. Measured verdicts: wherever a number exists, Python decides the verdict,
     not the model. A finished period gets hit or miss. An unfinished one gets
     on track, ahead or behind pace, always with the numbers. The model only
     judges promises with no number to check.
  3. Maruti: the parent's results ("…results issued by Suzuki…") now count as a
     press release, not Maruti's own results. It's tested, but the feed keeps
     the old label until the next ingest run.
  4. Run: TCS + HDFCBANK below.

  TCS: 2 measured (both behind pace), 1 not yet measurable

  1. Promise: margins → 26% to 28% ("the coming quarters")
  "we want to inch closer to our 26% to 28% band and we'll make all efforts to
  climb towards 26%." (Q3 FY26 call, p.23)
  Actual: EBIT margin (operating profit less depreciation) was 24.4% over the
  three quarters since that call (Mar, Jun, Sep 2026). Source: screener's
  quarterly table.
  Verdict: Behind pace. Computed in Python.

  2. Promise: exit operating margin → 25% plus
  "We want to exit at 25% plus and strive to achieve it sooner rather than
  later." (Q1 FY27 call, p.23)
  Actual: "Our operating margins remained stable sequentially at 24%." (Q2 FY27
  call, p.5)
  Verdict: Behind pace. Groq picked this quote as the evidence; Python compared
  24% with ≥25%.

  3. Promise: at least 1% of the employee base working in the new operating
  model ("going forward")
  "…target to have at least 1% of our employee base, to work in the new
  operating model." (Q1 FY27 call, p.14)
  Actual: not disclosed.
  Verdict: Not yet measurable. No later call reports this share. Judged by Groq.

  HDFCBANK: 3 promises, all not yet measurable

  1. Promise: outpace system loan growth in FY27
  "we believe and we are very optimistic about outpacing loan growth in the
  coming year in FY '27" (Q3 FY26 call, p.4)
  Actual: "September'26 EOP loan growth has grown 23.5% year-on-year compared to
  17% to 18% year-on-year growth levels" (Q2 FY27 call, p.4)
  Verdict: Not yet measurable. Groq: the target has no number, and the call
  doesn't say whether 17–18% is the system's growth.

  2. Promise: CD ratio → 85% to 90% for FY27
  "I think we should land somewhere around the 85% to 90% for FY '27." (Q3 FY26
  call, p.4)
  Actual: not disclosed.
  Verdict: Not yet measurable. Judged by Groq.

  3. Promise: CD ratio → 90% to 96% in FY26
  "we will try and be in a range of somewhere between 90% to 96% in the year FY
  '26" (Q3 FY26 call, p.6)
  Actual: not disclosed.
  Verdict: Not yet measurable. Judged by Groq.

  Why HDFCBANK has no measured verdicts: I searched all four HDFCBANK
  transcripts myself. After setting the CD-ratio target, management never states
  the ratio again; on the Q4 FY26 call they say "LDR is not in focus". Our rule
  allows only transcripts and annual reports on the research side, so these
  verdicts are honest, not a gap in extraction. The fix I'd suggest: read
  advances and deposits from the bank's own quarterly results filing (we already
  download those for the feed) and compute the CD ratio in Python. That would
  measure both CD-ratio promises. It means letting results filings count as a
  research source, so it's your call.

  Two other things to know:
  - The new extractor returns fewer, more checkable promises: TCS has 3 (was
    5–6), HDFCBANK 3 (was 6), so there are fewer verdicts per company.
  - Rewriting the prompts meant re-reading transcripts several times. All PvD
    runs so far cost $0.27 for 40 calls; total LLM spend is $0.46 for 270 calls.

  Stopping here as asked. Remember to /export this session into build-history/.
